- [x] *medium* Sub-leaf L1c — `std::optional<T>` → `rusty::Option<T>` (1 prod site, ~33 test sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30** (L1c-prod 2026-04-28, L1c-tests 2026-04-30).
  - **Survey finding (2026-04-28)**: 1 prod site (`src/srpc/reactor/reactor.h:mutable std::optional<OnReady> on_ready`); 33 test sites mostly under `std::optional<rusty::Arc<T>>` or `std::optional<ChannelConnectionProxy>` patterns. Tests use `*opt` and `opt.emplace(...)` heavily — patterns that don't translate cleanly to `rusty::Option` (whose `operator*` returns the Option itself, not the inner T, and which has no `emplace`).
  - 1:1 conversion: `std::optional<T>` → `rusty::Option<T>`; `std::nullopt` → `rusty::None`; `opt.has_value()` → `opt.is_some()`; `*opt` → `opt.as_ref().unwrap()` (read) or `opt.unwrap()` (consume); `opt = std::nullopt` → `opt = rusty::None`; `opt.emplace(...)` → `opt = rusty::Some(...)`.
  - **API drift caveats**:
    - `rusty::Option<T>::operator*` returns the Option itself (a transpilation shim for migrated rust code), not the inner T. Code that uses `*opt` for read access needs to switch to `opt.as_ref().unwrap()` or chain through some other accessor.
    - `rusty::Option<T>` doesn't have an `emplace(...)` method analogous to `std::optional::emplace`; sites need either `opt = rusty::Some(T(...))` or `opt = rusty::Some(rusty::Box<T>(...))` depending on whether `T` is movable.
  - Decompose:
    - [x] **L1c-prod — `src/srpc/reactor/reactor.h`** (1 site, 16 LOC). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**.
      - `mutable std::optional<OnReady> on_ready` (line 297) → `mutable rusty::Option<OnReady> on_ready`.
      - The TaskState ctor's member-init `on_ready(std::move(cb))` works as-is via `rusty::Option<T>::Option(T val)` implicit-from-T constructor.
      - Body changes: `state->on_ready.has_value()` → `state->on_ready.is_some()`; the two-step `auto cb = std::move(state->on_ready.value()); state->on_ready.reset();` collapses into a single `auto cb = state->on_ready.unwrap();` (Option::unwrap moves out and sets to None in one step, equivalent to Rust's `Option::take()` followed by unwrap).
      - Verification: `test_reactor` 15/15 (the dedicated reactor unit test), full RPC suite green: `test_rpc` 17/17, `test_rpc_extended` 14/14, `test_rpc_state_integration` 16/16, `test_rpc_request_queue` 30/30, `test_rpc_request_buffering` 8/8, `test_rpc_combined_reliability` 9/9, `test_rpc_reconnect_integration` 12/12, `test_load_balancer` 21/21, `test_rpc_client_pool` 20/20, `test_marshal` 23/23, full channel-layer suite 154/154. Same 4 pre-existing flakes elsewhere; none new.
    - [x] **L1c-tests** — 18 test files migrated. ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**. Patterns swept: `std::optional<rusty::Arc<T>>` / `std::optional<rusty::Box<T>>` / `std::optional<ChannelConnectionProxy>` test-fixture members → `rusty::Option<...>`; `opt.emplace(args)` → `opt = rusty::Some(args)`; `opt.reset()` / `opt = std::nullopt` → `opt = rusty::None`; `opt.has_value()` → `opt.is_some()`; `(*opt)->method()` → `opt.as_ref().unwrap()->method()`; `(*opt).clone()` → `opt.as_ref().unwrap().clone()`. Boundary care: `Arc::get()` is a method on `Arc` (not `T`), so `*conn_->get()` requires `*conn_.as_ref().unwrap().get()` (with `.` not `->`); `(*opt).clone()` for `Option<Arc<T>>` requires `opt.as_ref().unwrap().clone()` to clone the inner Arc, not the Option. Files: `rpc_{client,server}_channel_{binding,close,factory,recv,send}_test.cc` (10), `rpc_inmemory_channel{,_e2e}_test.cc` (2), `rpc_tcp_{channel,factory,listener}_test.cc` (3), `rpc_rocksdb_log_storage_test.cc`, `sharding_startup_test.cc`, `test_marshal.cc`. Verification: 18 migrated tests all green via `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`; full suite 102/107 with same 5 pre-existing failures (`test_config_client`, `test_rpc_timeout_retry`, `test_rpc_validation`, `test_rpc_metrics`, `rpcbench` path issue) that fail on stock mako-dev.
  - Goal achieved: zero `std::optional` in srpc **prod and tests** code post-L1c.

#### Targeted single-type migrations (decomposed by file)

- [x] *high* Sub-leaf L2 — `std::list<T>` → rusty equivalents (76 prod sites total). ✅ **LANDED across 2026-04-28 → 2026-05-01** — all 5 sub-leaves (L2a-base + L2b-request_queue + L2c-alock + L2d-server-cleanup + L2e-recorder) done.  srpc's prod tree no longer has any non-carve-out `std::list` left; the four documented carve-outs (`misc/marshal.hpp`, `rpc/idempotency.hpp`, `rpc/completion_tracker.hpp`, `misc/alock.{hpp,cpp}`) all stay std::list with recorded rationale below.
  - **Survey refinement (2026-04-28)**: of the 76 prod-side `std::list` mentions, only ~36 are actual code uses; the other ~40 are stale safety annotations in `@external:` comments and `@unsafe` documentation that reference RequestQueue / std::list. Breakdown:
    - `base/threading.hpp` (2 actual sites) — Queue<T> FIFO with O(1) push_back / pop_front / front + cond_var. ✅ **MIGRATED to `rusty::VecDeque<T>`** (correct target for FIFO queue with both-end O(1) access; not `Vec` since Vec has no pop_front).
    - `misc/alock.{hpp,cpp}` (22 actual sites) — TBD; async lock waiter queues, likely benefit from VecDeque or another structure.
    - `rpc/request_queue.hpp` (19 actual sites) — push_back/pop_front queue, **migrate to `rusty::VecDeque`**, NOT Vec (front-pop is O(n) on Vec).
    - `misc/recorder.{hpp,cpp}` (8 actual sites) — raw `std::list<io_req_t*>*` heap pointer pattern; needs design rework before migration. Defer.
    - `misc/marshal.hpp` (5 sites) — **public marshal API**: `operator<<(Marshal&, const std::list<T>&)` etc. Removing breaks user code that serializes std::list across the wire. **Carve-out** — stays std::list. Could ADD parallel `rusty::Vec<T>` / `rusty::VecDeque<T>` overloads; would not change anything else.
    - `rpc/server.{hpp,cpp}` (14 sites) — **all in `@external:` safety annotation comments** (`std::list::push_back: [safe...]`, etc.). No actual std::list types or methods in production code. These are stale annotations; cleanup task = delete the dead annotations.
    - `rpc/client.hpp` (4 sites) — all in `@unsafe - Uses RequestQueue which uses std::list` comments. Become stale once request_queue is migrated.
    - `rpc/idempotency.hpp` (1 site) — LRU cache uses `std::list::splice` for O(1) iterator-stable node moves, with `HashMap<Key, list::iterator>` cache. **Carve-out** — Vec/VecDeque can't replace splice + iterator-stable storage without redesigning the LRU.
    - `rpc/completion_tracker.hpp` (1 site) — same iterator-stability pattern (uses `erase(iterator)` returned from external storage). **Carve-out** for the same reason.
  - Decomposed:
    - [x] **L2a-base — `src/srpc/base/threading.hpp`** (2 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**.
      - `rusty::Box<std::list<T>> q_` → `rusty::Box<rusty::VecDeque<T>> q_`.
      - API mappings: `q_->push_back(x)` unchanged (VecDeque has push_back); `q_->empty()` → `q_->is_empty()`; `auto x = std::move(q_->front()); q_->pop_front();` → `auto x = q_->pop_front();` (VecDeque's pop_front returns the popped T, simplifying the move-then-destroy pattern).
      - Verification: `test_rpc` 17/17, `test_rpc_extended` 14/14, `test_rpc_state_integration` 16/16, `test_rpc_combined_reliability` 9/9, `test_rpc_request_buffering` 8/8, `test_rpc_reconnect_integration` 12/12, `test_load_balancer` 21/21, `test_rpc_client_pool` 20/20, `test_marshal` 23/23, full channel-layer suite green. Same 4 pre-existing flakes — none new.
    - [x] **L2b-request_queue — `src/srpc/rpc/request_queue.hpp`** (19 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**. Migrated `std::list<QueuedRequest>` → `rusty::VecDeque<QueuedRequest>`.
      - API mappings: `queue_.empty()` → `queue_.is_empty()`; `auto x = std::move(queue_.front()); queue_.pop_front();` simplified to `auto x = queue_.pop_front();` (VecDeque's `pop_front` returns `T`, no need for explicit move-then-destroy); `queue_.size()` / `front()` / `push_back(...)` / `clear()` / range-based-for iteration all work unchanged (VecDeque exposes random-access `iterator`); `erase(iterator)` rewritten via `extract_if(predicate)` returning a `VecDeque<T>` of expired entries that the caller drains via `pop_front` to invoke callbacks (the `rusty::Function<bool(const T&)>` predicate can't move out of the element so the drain is a separate pass).
      - **Same in-place construction property as `std::list`**: VecDeque's ring-buffer uses placement new on contiguous slots; QueuedRequest's `Marshal`-bearing payload is constructed once on push_back and only moved on container grow / `make_contiguous()` rotates — same "no move-assignment after enqueue" property the prior std::list comment cited (now updated in-line).
      - Verification: `test_rpc_request_queue` 30/30, full RPC suite green: `test_rpc` 17/17, `test_rpc_extended` 14/14, `test_rpc_state_integration` 16/16, `test_rpc_combined_reliability` 9/9, `test_rpc_request_buffering` 8/8, `test_rpc_reconnect_integration` 12/12, `test_load_balancer` 21/21, `test_rpc_client_pool` 20/20, `test_marshal` 23/23, full channel-layer suite 154/154. Same 4 pre-existing flakes elsewhere; none new.
    - [x] **L2c-alock — `src/srpc/misc/alock.{hpp,cpp}`** (22 sites). ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-05-01 as a documented carve-out**, matching the existing precedent for `rpc/idempotency.hpp` and `rpc/completion_tracker.hpp`.
      - **Decision**: alock's `std::list<lock_req_t> requests_` (the lock-waiter queue) keeps `std::list` storage.  The rationale matches the existing idempotency.hpp / completion_tracker.hpp carve-outs: alock's algorithm relies on three standard linked-list properties that `rusty::VecDeque` cannot provide without semantic-level refactoring:
        - **Reverse iteration** (alock.cpp:309, 329) — `requests_.rbegin()` / `requests_.rend()` walk the wait-die priority list backwards; `VecDeque` exposes only forward iterators.
        - **Iterator-stable erase-during-iterate** (alock.cpp:230, 257, 270, 437, 459, 471) — `requests_.erase(it)` returns the next iterator and leaves all other iterators / pointers valid; `VecDeque::erase(iterator)` does not exist, and the `extract_if(predicate)` substitute can't move out of elements (its `Function<bool(const T&)>` predicate is by-const-ref).
        - **Pointer-stable element access across method calls** — `read_acquire(rusty::Vec<lock_req_t *> &)` consumes pointers obtained via `&(*it)` from earlier list iteration; it then mutates `req->status` and copies `req->yes_callback` (now Arc clone via the wrapper).  In `std::list`, push_back never invalidates pointers; in `VecDeque`, a push_back triggered by a re-entrant `lock()` call from inside a yes_callback would reallocate the ring buffer and invalidate every captured pointer (a real risk because user callbacks routinely re-enter alock through `lock_sync` from deptran 2pl/scheduler, etc.).
      - **Why a refactor is not pursued here**: the alternative — refactor the lock-waiter queue's storage + every iterator-stable callsite (~80-200 LOC, plus careful re-derivation of the wait-die invariants) — would carry real correctness risk for limited benefit, since `lock_req_t` itself is now small (its callback fields migrated to the Arc<Function const> wrapper under L5-alock; lock_req_t is no longer the owner of expensive std::function payloads).  The std::list-driven node allocation cost was the original concern with std::list but it is uncontended (alock is single-shard / single-threaded internally — see L7-alock removal of dead mutex fields).
      - **Action**: added a short carve-out comment block above `std::list<lock_req_t> requests_;` in alock.hpp (both `WaitDieALock` and `TimeoutALock`) recording this decision and pointing future readers at the iterator-stability rationale; the carve-out is now also recorded in the L2 parent's "Carve-outs" list below alongside marshal.hpp / idempotency.hpp / completion_tracker.hpp.
      - **No code change beyond the comment** — std::list stays.  This formally closes L2c-alock and unblocks the L2 parent.
    - [x] **L2d-server-cleanup — `src/srpc/rpc/server.{hpp,cpp}` + `client.hpp`** (~18 stale-annotation sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**.
      - Deleted 6 stale `std::list::*: [safe...]` annotation lines from `server.hpp`'s `@external:` block (and the trailing `std::__cxx11::list::push_back` line); deleted 9 stale `std::list::*: [safe]` annotation lines from `server.cpp`'s `@external:` block. No code change — `server.{hpp,cpp}` had no actual `std::list` types or method calls in production code; the annotations were stale leftovers from before a prior migration retired the std::list usage.
      - Updated 4 `client.hpp` comments from `// @unsafe - Uses RequestQueue which uses std::list` to `// @unsafe - Uses RequestQueue (backed by rusty::VecDeque)` to reflect the L2b migration that already landed.
      - Verification: `test_rpc` 17/17, `test_rpc_extended` 14/14, `test_rpc_state_integration` 16/16, `test_rpc_request_queue` 30/30, `test_rpc_request_buffering` 8/8, `test_rpc_combined_reliability` 9/9 — annotation-only changes with no behavioral risk.
    - [x] **L2e-recorder — `src/srpc/misc/recorder.{hpp,cpp}`** ✅ **OBSOLETE 2026-04-30** — `Recorder` class deleted in Workstream N Phase 4e-36 (was unused after Phases 4e-33/4e-34/4e-35 removed all `recorder_` fields).  The 8 `std::list` sites went away with the class.  Verified by `ls src/srpc/misc/recorder.*` returning no matches.
  - Carve-outs (stay std::list, with recorded rationale):
    - `misc/marshal.hpp` (5 sites) — public marshal API; removing breaks user serialization. ADD rusty equivalents alongside if useful, never remove std::list overloads.
    - `rpc/idempotency.hpp` (1 site) — splice-based LRU cache; needs LRU redesign before VecDeque can replace.
    - `rpc/completion_tracker.hpp` (1 site) — iterator-stable cache; same constraint.
    - `misc/alock.{hpp,cpp}` (22 sites) — wait-die lock-waiter queue with reverse iteration, iterator-stable erase-during-iterate, and pointer-stable element access across re-entrant `lock()` calls.  See L2c-alock above for the full rationale.
  - Per-leaf verification: full RPC-focused suite green.
  - Goal: zero non-carve-out `std::list` in srpc prod code after L2b–L2e land. Marshalling overloads keep std::list as a serialization-level interop type only.

#### Bigger migrations (require decomposition into multiple leaves each)

- [x] *medium* Sub-leaf L4 — `std::map` / `std::unordered_map` → `rusty::HashMap<K,V>` / `rusty::BTreeMap<K,V>` in srpc prod code. ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**.
  - **Survey refinement (2026-04-28)**: of the 48 prod-side mentions, only 7 are actual code uses — 1 in `inmemory_channel.hpp`'s `InMemorySwitchboard::listeners_` field, 6 in `marshal.hpp`'s public `operator<<` / `operator>>` overloads (carve-out: stays std for user serialization compat). The remaining 41 are stale `@external:` / `@unsafe` annotation comments scattered across `client.{hpp,cpp}`, `server.{hpp,cpp}`, `alarm.hpp`, `alock.cpp`, `base/misc.hpp`, `marshal.cpp`. The fields they document were already migrated to `rusty::HashMap` / `rusty::BTreeMap` long before but the annotations were never updated.
  - **Done in this leaf**:
    - Migrated `InMemorySwitchboard::listeners_` from `std::unordered_map<std::string, rusty::sync::Weak<InMemoryListener>>` to `rusty::HashMap<std::string, rusty::sync::Weak<InMemoryListener>>`. API mappings: `find(key) != end()` → `contains_key(key)`; `find(key) + it->second.upgrade()` → `get(key) + val_opt.unwrap()->upgrade()` (Option<V*> + pointer deref); `emplace(key, val)` → `insert(key, val)`; `erase(key)` → `remove(key)`; `erase(iterator)` → not used after migration (replaced with `remove(addr)`).
    - Cleaned up stale annotations in `client.hpp` (8 `std::unordered_map` + 5 `std::map` lines + 1 `// @unsafe - Uses SpinMutex + std::unordered_map access` comment now reading `rusty::HashMap`), `server.hpp` (8 `std::unordered_map` lines), `server.cpp` (4 `std::unordered_map` lines), `client.cpp` (6 `std::map` / `std::unordered_map` lines), `alarm.hpp` (2 comments updated to reference `rusty::HashMap`), `alock.cpp` (1 line), `base/misc.hpp` (1 line, made template-generic), `marshal.cpp` (1 comment updated to reference `rusty::HashMap::get`).
  - **Carve-out (stays std)**: `misc/marshal.hpp` `operator<<` / `operator>>` overloads for `std::map<K,V>` / `std::unordered_map<K,V>` — public marshal API; same rationale as the `std::list`/`std::set` carve-outs.
  - Verification (regression-free): `test_rpc` 17/17, `test_rpc_extended` 14/14, `test_rpc_state_integration` 16/16, `test_rpc_request_buffering` 8/8, `test_rpc_combined_reliability` 9/9, `test_rpc_inmemory_channel` 24/24 (the dedicated suite that exercises the migrated `InMemorySwitchboard`), `test_rpc_inmemory_channel_e2e` 4/4, `test_marshal` 23/23, `test_load_balancer` 21/21, `test_reactor` 15/15, `test_rpc_request_queue` 30/30.
  - Goal: zero non-carve-out `std::map` / `std::unordered_map` in srpc **prod** code achieved.
- [x] *medium* Sub-leaf L5 — `std::function<F>` → `rusty::Function<F>` (143 prod sites, 24 test sites). ✅ **LANDED across 2026-04-28 → 2026-05-01** — all 24 sub-leaves (L5a + L5b…L5p + L5q…L5t + L5-callbacks + L5-channel + L5-client-attr + L5-alock) done.  Pattern landed: callbacks where the surrounding storage / API requires copyability (channel.hpp typedefs, FutureAttr propagation through rcc_rpc.h, alock's lock_req_t, callbacks.hpp's Vec<std::function>::clone() drains) use the `detail::CallbackWrapper<Sig>` Arc<Function const>-backed thin facade in `base/callback_wrapper.hpp`; everything else uses `rusty::Function<...>` directly.
  - **Semantic concern**: `rusty::Function` is move-only by default; `std::function` is copyable. Sites that store callbacks in containers requiring copyability (e.g. `Vec<std::function<...>>::clone()`) need either container migration first (L2) or explicit `Arc<Function<F>>` wrapping. Audit for copy-in-storage sites before starting.
  - **Decomposed**:
    - [x] **L5a — `heartbeat.hpp` + `alarm.hpp`** (6 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**.
      - `heartbeat.hpp` (3 sites): `std::function<void()> on_timeout_` → `rusty::Function<void()> on_timeout_`; setter param updated; stale `// @unsafe - std::function::operator bool` comment updated to reference `rusty::Function::operator bool`. Single-shot callback fired on heartbeat timeout — never copied; clean migration.
      - `alarm.hpp` (3 sites): `std::pair<uint64_t, std::function<void(void)>>` value type in `BTreeMap<uint64_t, ...> waiting_` → uses `rusty::Function<void(void)>` instead; same for `idx_time_` map; `add(...)` parameter type updated. The pre-existing `auto item = *it;` pattern is safe under move-only-V because `BTreeMap<K,V>::iterator::operator*()` returns `std::pair<const K&, V&>` by value (a pair-of-references constructed in place; not a copy of V).
      - Verification: `test_rpc_heartbeat` 20/20 (the dedicated heartbeat suite directly exercises the migrated callback storage), full RPC suite green.
    - [x] **L5-callbacks — `rpc/callbacks.hpp` ConnectionCallbacks Vec<std::function> drains + std::mutex** (3 type aliases + 5 add_*/invoke_* + 5 size methods + std::mutex blast-radius). ✅ **LANDED 2026-05-01**.
      - Picked option (a) Arc-wrap from the prior design discussion: `using ConnectionCallback = rusty::Arc<rusty::Function<void() const>>;` (similarly for `ErrorCallback` over `void(RpcError, const std::string&)` and `ReconnectCallback` over `void(bool)`).  The `const` qualifier on Function lets it be invoked through `const Arc&` (via `(*arc)(args)`), and the typical non-mutable `[&]`/`[this]`/`[=]` lambdas the suite uses are const-callable with no caller-side change.
      - `add_*` methods now take `rusty::Function<...const>` by value (auto-converts from a plain lambda), wrap with `ConnectionCallback::make(std::move(cb))`, then push into the per-event Vec under lock. `invoke_*` methods clone the per-event Vec under lock (Arc copy = atomic refcount bump, no callback copy), drop the lock, and dereference each `Arc<Function const>` via `(*cb)(args...)` inside `invoke_safely`. Behavior is identical to the std::function pattern (clone-out-of-lock, invoke-without-lock) but with cheap Arc clones rather than std::function deep copies.
      - **Blast-radius migration in the same commit**: `mutable std::mutex mutex_; mutable ConnectionCallbacks callbacks_;` → `mutable srpc::SpinMutex<ConnectionCallbacks> callbacks_;` (data-inside-the-mutex). Every `std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(mutex_); /* use callbacks_ */` collapses to `auto guard = callbacks_.lock().unwrap(); /* use guard-> */`. This closes L7-callbacks (the std::mutex side) in the same commit since the two were structurally tied.
      - `Client` wrapper methods (`client.hpp` add_on_connected/disconnected/error/reconnecting/reconnected) updated to take `rusty::Function<...const>` rather than the old `ConnectionCallback`/`ErrorCallback`/`ReconnectCallback` (std::function) parameter — same auto-conversion from lambdas, no caller-side change for any of the 59 external (test-only) call sites in `rpc_inmemory_channel_e2e_test.cc`, `rpc_state_integration_test.cc`, `rpc_client_channel_close_test.cc`, `rpc_callbacks_test.cc`, `rpc_docs_symbols_test.cc`. (Production code outside srpc/ does not register lifecycle callbacks.)
      - `invoke_safely` is now templated on `Callback` (the Arc<Function const>-shaped element type), invoking via `(*cb)(args...)` — first deref Arc → Function const&, then Function's const operator() runs the user callable. Exception handling is unchanged (`try { ... } catch (...) { /* swallow */ }`).
      - Added `#include <rusty/function.hpp>` and `#include "../base/threading.hpp"` to `callbacks.hpp`.
      - Verification: full RPC suite via `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`. The dedicated `rpc_callbacks_test`, `rpc_state_integration_test`, and `rpc_inmemory_channel_e2e_test` exercise every register + invoke path. 101/107 tests passed; the same 4 pre-existing baseline failures (`test_rpc_timeout_retry`, `test_rpc_validation`, `test_rpc_metrics`, `rpcbench` path issue) and 2 host-overload transient failures (`test_rpc_docs_snippet_compile`, `test_rpc_rpcgen_compile` — total runtime 1170s vs typical 150s, multiple `snippet timed out during compile / timeout_sec: 60.0` entries — these surface whenever the host is loaded; previously documented in L5o entry). No new failures from this change.
    - [x] **L5o-request-queue — `rpc/request_queue.hpp` QueuedRequest::callback + Vec<Function> drains** (5 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-05-01**.
      - Migrated `QueuedRequest::callback` field from `std::function<void(int)>` to `rusty::Function<void(int)>`. The two `Vec<std::function<void(int)>> callbacks_to_invoke` locals (in `expire_stale` and `clear_all`) likewise updated to `Vec<rusty::Function<void(int)>>`.
      - **Dropped `RequestQueue::peek(QueuedRequest&)`** — its `out = guard->front();` copy-assignment relied on QueuedRequest being copyable, which is no longer the case after the callback field went move-only. The method had no production callers (tests-only, used to inspect xid post-enqueue); equivalent inspection is done via `size()`/`empty()` (and dequeue, which moves out). Replaced the prior `Peek` and `PeekEmptyReturnsFalse` test cases with `EnqueueIncreasesSize` and `EmptyQueueDequeueReturnsNone` to preserve coverage of the size-tracking and empty-dequeue contracts without the copy.
      - Iteration over `callbacks_to_invoke` switched from `for (const auto& cb : ...)` to `for (auto& cb : ...)` — `rusty::Function::operator()` is non-const for non-const-callable specializations, so the loop variable can't be `const`. The semantic is identical (we don't move out, we invoke).
      - Added `#include <rusty/function.hpp>` to request_queue.hpp.
      - The drain pattern (move callbacks out under lock, invoke outside lock) translates cleanly: `callbacks_to_invoke.push(std::move(req.callback));` already moved, and the post-lock invocation works on a vector of move-only Functions.
      - Verification: `test_rpc_request_queue` 30/30 (the dedicated suite that exercises enqueue/dequeue/expire_stale/clear_all/size/empty paths).         - `using ALockLockedCallback = detail::CallbackWrapper<void(uint64_t) const>;` (vlock fires with the lock id)
        - `using ALockNotifyCallback = detail::CallbackWrapper<void() const>;` (public lock's yes/no callbacks)
        - `using ALockWoundCallback  = detail::CallbackWrapper<int() const>;`

Those are independent of the callback type and remain a separate carve-out / future leaf decision per the L2c-alock note above.  By migrating callbacks via the wrapper, we unblock L7-alock (the std::mutex side of alock) and let L2c-alock decision happen independently.

    - [x] **L5d-runlater — `base/threading.{hpp,cpp}` RunLater raw-ptr job heap** (6 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**.
      - Migrated `typedef std::pair<double, std::function<void()>*> job_t` → `typedef std::pair<double, rusty::Option<rusty::Box<rusty::Function<void()>>>> job_t`. The `Option::None` payload encodes the former `nullptr` death-pill; `Option::Some(Box<Function>)` owns the heap-allocated callable that was previously raw-`new`'d.
      - Death-pill push: `make_pair(0.0, nullptr)` → `job_t(0.0, rusty::None)`.
      - Insert: `make_pair(later, new std::function<void()>(f))` → `job_t(later, rusty::Some(rusty::make_box<rusty::Function<void()>>(std::move(f))))`.
      - API change: `int run_later(double, const std::function<void()>&)` → `int run_later(double, rusty::Function<void()>)` (by-value with move).
      - `try_one_job` algorithm: replaced "peek-by-copy then pop" with "move-out then pop". Move out the Option payload from `jobs_.front().second` *before* `std::pop_heap`, then `jobs_.pop()` removes the now-empty back slot left by pop_heap's swap. Death-pill detection becomes `job_func.is_none()`; live-job dispatch becomes `auto box = std::move(job_func).unwrap(); (*box)();` (Box drops at end of scope, freeing the Function — replacing the prior `delete job_func`).
      - Added a file-local `GreaterByJobTime` functor for the heap comparator. The prior `std::greater<job_t>{}` transitively required `operator<` on the second pair element, which `Option<Box<Function>>` does not provide. The new comparator orders only by `pair.first` (the wall-clock timestamp), which is what the heap actually needs — two jobs with identical timestamps were never an ordering concern.
      - **Zero active callers**: `grep -rE "RunLater\b" src/ | grep -v threading` returns nothing — the class is fully self-contained within `threading.{hpp,cpp}` and never instantiated by any other unit. API change has zero caller-side migration cost.

    - [x] **L5f-reactor-poll — `reactor/reactor.{h,cc}` stackless poll_once** (4 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**.
      - Migrated `StacklessTaskEntry::poll_once` from `std::function<bool(rusty::Context&)>` → `rusty::Function<bool(rusty::Context&)>`. The `Reactor::register_stackless_poller(...)` parameter was changed from `std::function` to `rusty::Function` (by-value with move).
      - **`process_stackless_tasks` algorithm**: replaced "copy poll_fn out under lock, drop lock, call" with "move poll_fn out under lock, drop lock, call, re-acquire lock and put it back if poll didn't return Ready". The previous std::function-copy pattern relied on copyability — moot for rusty::Function. The new move-out/move-back pattern is safe because the reactor is single-threaded: any synchronous waker callback during poll only mutates the slot's `queued`/`active` flags (never `poll_once`), and the put-back happens before the next ready-queue iteration would re-pop the slot.
      - Both call sites of `register_stackless_poller` (`reactor.cc:736` and `reactor.h:306`) pass `[state](rusty::Context& ctx) mutable { ... }` lambdas, which auto-convert into `rusty::Function` via its `Function(Callable&&)` constructor — no caller-side change needed.
      - Added `#include <rusty/fn.hpp>` to reactor.h.
      - Verification: `test_reactor` and `test_future` green (the suites that exercise the stackless task path most heavily). Full RPC suite green: 102/107 with same 4 pre-existing failures (`test_rpc_timeout_retry`, `test_rpc_validation`, `test_rpc_metrics`, `rpcbench` path issue) as on stock mako-dev; no new failures.
    - [x] **L5g-onetimejob — `base/misc.hpp` OneTimeJob func_** (3 sites). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**.
      - Migrated `OneTimeJob::func_` from `std::function<void()>` to `rusty::Function<void()>`. Constructor signature `OneTimeJob(std::function<void()> func)` → `OneTimeJob(rusty::Function<void()> func)` (still by-value), with the body changed from `func_(func)` (copy) to `func_(std::move(func))`.
      - Removed the redundant user-declared destructor `virtual ~OneTimeJob(){};`. With a user-defined destructor present, the compiler suppresses implicit move-ctor synthesis (rule of five) — that worked when `func_` was copyable std::function but breaks when it's move-only rusty::Function. The base class `Job::~Job() = default;` is virtual and covers polymorphic deletion; OneTimeJob doesn't need its own destructor.

The lambda auto-converts to rusty::Function via the converting `Function(Callable&&)` constructor. The Arc-of-rvalue-OneTimeJob construction calls the implicitly-generated move constructor (now available after removing the explicit destructor). No caller-side change needed.

- [ ] *medium* Sub-leaf L6 — `std::shared_ptr<T>` → `rusty::Arc<T>` (multi-thread) or `rusty::Rc<T>` (single-thread). **DEFERRED with documented findings (2026-04-28)**: the 87 prod sites split into two distinct islands, each blocked.
  - **Survey breakdown (2026-04-28)**:
    - **Island A — `misc/marshal.hpp` (38 sites)**: `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>` is the carrier type for the public marshal/serialization API. It flows through `MarshallableSharedPtrAdapter`, `TypedMarshallableAdapter`, `MarshallDeputy::sp_data_`/`inner_sp_data_`, `wrap_typed_marshallable<T>(...)`, `make_marshallable_proxy(...)`, `marshallable_proxy_inner(...)`, and the `pro::proxy` convention `add_convention<MarshallableMemInner, std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>() const>`.
    - **Island B — Event subsystem (49 sites)**: `std::shared_ptr<Event>` / `<IntEvent>` / `<BoxEvent<T>>` is the core ownership type for `Reactor::all_events_`/`waiting_events_`/`timeout_events_`/`composite_events_` queues, `WaitAll`/`WaitAny`/`WaitN`/`SharedIntEvent` containers, `Event::set_self()`/`get_self()`, `FiberFuture::state_`, `FiberChannel::pending_recv_event_`. The 2 `std::weak_ptr<Event>` sites (L1a) are the weak-side of this same chain.
  - **Why Phase-1-internal-only refactor is INFEASIBLE**: `rusty::Arc<T>` and `std::shared_ptr<T>` use **separate, incompatible refcount control blocks**. There is no way to take a `std::shared_ptr<T>` argument and "wrap it as Arc<T>" internally — the data was allocated and ref-counted by std::shared_ptr's machinery, and Arc cannot adopt it without a new heap allocation that creates two independent refcounts (one in shared_ptr, one in Arc) on the same object — a recipe for double-free or premature destruction. So the migration is all-or-nothing on each public API surface.
  - **Island A blocker — public marshal API**: migrating to `rusty::Arc<Marshallable>` means changing the **boundary type** of:
    - `MarshallDeputy(std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>)` ctor
    - `MarshallDeputy::set_marshallable(std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>)` setter
    - `make_marshallable_proxy(std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>)` factory
    - `marshallable_proxy_inner(...) -> std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>` accessor
    - `MarInitializerState::marshallable` / `proxy` fields (returned by initializer factories)
    
    Every external user that constructs a `std::make_shared<MyMarshallable>()` and passes it to a MarshallDeputy / make_marshallable_proxy needs to migrate too. Survey of external callers (deptran/raft + deptran/classic):
    - `deptran/raft/log_storage.hpp:55,67` — `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable> command` is a **field of LogEntry** (the on-disk replicated log entry struct). Migration changes the persisted log structure type, touching every raft replay/persistence/rocksdb-storage path.
    - `deptran/raft/testconf.{h,cc}` — `std::map<siteid_t, std::function<int(int, std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>)>> commit_callbacks` is a public callback signature stored in a std::function<>'d std::map.
    - `deptran/raft/service.cc:100`, `deptran/raft/transport.hpp:29` — local `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable> cmd` and references in transport.
    - **`deptran/classic/scheduler.cc:375`** — uses a **non-owning shared_ptr with a no-op deleter**: `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>(&tpc_commit_cmd, [](Marshallable*) {})`. This pattern uses `std::shared_ptr<T>` as a "type-erased non-owning ref" where the underlying object lives elsewhere. **`rusty::Arc` has no equivalent** — Arc always owns and refcount-frees. Resolving this requires either (a) changing the receiving API to take `Marshallable*`/`Marshallable&`, (b) heap-allocating a copy and giving Arc real ownership, or (c) keeping `std::shared_ptr` here as a documented carve-out for non-owning views.
  - **Island B blocker — Event subsystem coupling**: `std::weak_ptr<T>` only interoperates with `std::shared_ptr<T>`, so migrating `weak_ptr<Event>` (L1a's 2 sites) forces migrating the entire `shared_ptr<Event>` chain. The `event.h` header has explicit `@external:` safety annotations marking std::shared_ptr operations as a documented unsafe boundary; this island is intentionally a single atomic refactor unit. Plus `Event::set_self()`/`get_self()` is a `enable_shared_from_this`-style pattern that needs translation to the codebase's `Weak<T>` adapter.
  - **Plan refresh (2026-05-01)** — split Island A into a real, decomposed migration of the **deptran (user-of-srpc) side**, keeping the srpc framework's wire-boundary `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>` carve-out in `MarshallDeputy` itself.  Goal: deptran code no longer mentions `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>` directly; it carries either `MarshallDeputy` (the wire-format wrapper that already encapsulates the shared_ptr) or a typed `SerializableProxy` / concrete Serializable.  The conversion happens at the srpc boundary inside `MarshallDeputy`, exactly as CLAUDE.md prescribes ("Convert at the edge; isolate the conversion in one spot; annotate the boundary `@unsafe`").
    - **L6-A1 — `deptran/classic/scheduler.cc:381` non-owning `shared_ptr` workaround.** ✅ **LANDED 2026-05-01**: the function takes `Marshallable&`, the prior body wrapped it in a no-op-deleter `std::shared_ptr` to call `marshallable_cast<TpcCommitCommand>(shared_ptr)`.  Replaced by the existing `marshallable_cast<T>(Marshallable&)` overload (declared in `marshal.hpp:430`), which is the canonical reference-based API.  No semantic change.  The original "needs API redesign" note in the L6 finding was wrong — the reference overload was always available.

Same 4 pre-existing baseline failures (`test_rpc_timeout_retry`, `test_rpc_validation`, `test_rpc_metrics`, `rpcbench` path issue) and 2 host-overload transient compile-test timeouts (`test_rpc_docs_snippet_compile`, `test_rpc_rpcgen_compile` — total runtime 1395s vs typical 150s); no new failures.

- [x] *medium* Sub-leaf L7 — `std::mutex` + `std::lock_guard` → `srpc::SpinMutex<T>` / `rusty::Mutex<T>` ownership-style. ✅ **LANDED across 2026-04-28 → 2026-05-01** — all 8 sub-leaves (L7-request_queue + L7-inmemory_switchboard + L7-inmemory_connstate + L7-inmemory_listener + L7-callbacks + L7-alock + L7-recorder + L7-marshal + L7-cleanup) done.  srpc's prod tree no longer has any non-carve-out `std::mutex` / `std::recursive_mutex` left — every active mutex site is now `SpinMutex<Inner>` (data-inside-the-mutex pattern) or has been deleted as dead code.
  - **Semantic concern**: `rusty::Mutex<T>` and `srpc::SpinMutex<T>` *own* the protected data (Rust-style "data inside the mutex"), unlike `std::mutex` which is a separate primitive. Most sites need refactoring of the surrounding fields, not just a rename. Pick `Mutex<T>` for blocking/long-held locks; `SpinMutex<T>` for short critical sections (the srpc/rpc layer uses SpinMutex extensively).

Per CLAUDE.md: when touching any srpc file outside this workstream, *also* migrate STL constructs in the immediate blast radius of the change in the same commit (mention each migration in the commit message). The opportunistic-migration channel keeps the survey baseline shrinking between dedicated workstream commits.

     - `src/srpc/rpc/client.cpp` — biggest concentration (~12 sites).  Covers three distinct API shifts: (a) BTreeMap iter is now `tuple<const K&, V&>` (used in 4 places — destructor, `total_client_count`, `reconnect_all`, etc. — now use structured bindings); (b) `BTreeMap::get()` returns `Option<V&>` (a reference) instead of `Option<V*>` (a pointer), so `auto* clients = clients_opt.unwrap()` flips to `auto& clients = clients_opt.unwrap()` and all subsequent `clients->X()` / `*clients` flip to `clients.X()` / `clients` (8 sites); (c) `BTreeMap::keys()` returns a transient `keys_range` instead of `Vec<K>` — drained into a Vec via a for-loop iterator pattern.  Note: C++ `while (init; cond)` is NOT valid (only `if`/`switch` got init-form in C++17), so the drain uses `for (auto opt = it.next(); opt.is_some(); opt = it.next())`.
  3. **Reactor.cc** is now also unblocked — its `BTreeSet<rusty::Arc<srpc::Job>>` instantiation no longer hits the upstream BTreeSet bug.  No mako-side changes needed there.

## Workstream N: Marshal Archive Refactor (P2)

### Goal
Replace the current `Marshallable` + `MarshallDeputy` + `Marshal` + `MarshallableProxy` design with a serde / cereal-style architecture that decouples **format** (how bytes are laid out) from **target** (where bytes go).

### Design rationale
- **Buffer-vs-target coupling**: `Marshal::write_to_fd(int fd, size_t)` conflates "I am a buffer of bytes" with "I drain myself to an fd". Sending bytes over a TCP socket or hashing them requires copying through Marshal first.
- **Redundant `pro::proxy` over virtual dispatch**: `Marshallable` is already abstract with virtual methods. Wrapping it in a second `pro::proxy` layer (`MarshallableFacade` + `MarshallableSharedPtrAdapter` + `PRO_DEF_MEM_DISPATCH` macros) adds zero functionality. `MarshallDeputy` mirrors the same data twice (`sp_data_` + `inner_sp_data_`).
- **Save-and-load split**: each command type writes a separate `to_marshal(Marshal&)` AND `from_marshal(Marshal&)` — duplicate field-walking code per type. Serde / cereal collapse these into a single archive-templated `serialize` method.

### Architecture (4 layers, each independently composable)
1. **Sink / Source** (Layer 1+2): `SinkProxy` (`void write(const void*, size_t)`) and `SourceProxy` (`size_t read(void*, size_t)`) are `pro::proxy` facades. Concrete impls: `BufferSink`, `FdSink`, `TcpSink`, `HashSink`, `BufferSource`, `FdSource`, `TcpSource`.
2. **Archive** (Layer 3): `BinaryWriteArchive` / `BinaryReadArchive` know the wire format, hold a Sink/Source proxy, expose `operator<<` / `operator>>` for primitives + dispatch to user types' `serialize` method.
3. **Serializable trait** (Layer 4): `pro::proxy<SerializableFacade>` + `SerializableRegistry::reg<T>(kind)` factory registry replace `Marshallable` + `MarshallDeputy::reg_initializer<T>`.

### Reference design
- **Rust serde**: `Serialize` / `Deserialize` traits, format-agnostic, monomorphic per type. Sink is anything implementing `std::io::Write`. Polymorphism over heterogeneous command sets via tagged enums (closed) or `Box<dyn Trait>` + registry (open).
- **C++ cereal**: archive-templated single-method `template<class Archive> void serialize(Archive& ar)`, `cereal::BinaryOutputArchive` writes to `std::ostream`-shaped sinks, `CEREAL_REGISTER_TYPE` factory registry.

Full design: see [`docs/dev/marshal_archive_design.md`](dev/marshal_archive_design.md).

### Wire format compatibility
**The byte layout must remain unchanged**. Every existing wire-format test must continue to pass byte-for-byte through every phase. Phase 1 includes byte-for-byte regression tests against the existing `Marshal` for every primitive + composite.

    - [x] **Phase 3d-1 — Receive header decode via `BufferSource` + `BinaryReadArchive`** ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-29**. `ClientConnection::decode_response_and_notify` (the channel-mode reply demux) previously wrapped the inbound bytes in a temporary `Marshal body`, drained xid/error_code/server_instance_id through `Marshal::operator>>`, then chunk-shared the payload tail into the matching Future's `reply_` Marshal via `read_from_marshal`. Migrated the header parse off the temp Marshal: `BufferSource src(bytes, size)` + `BinaryReadArchive ar(&src)` parses the three header fields directly from the input bytes; the payload tail (if any) is byte-copied into `fu->reply_` via a single `Marshal::write(bytes + parsed_header_size, response_payload_bytes)`. `parsed_header_size` now derives from `src.pos()` instead of summing each field's `val_size()`. The defensive `if (size < parsed_header_size) → invoke_error_callback(EPROTO)` check became dead — `BufferSource::read` bounds reads to `size`, so any short read aborts inside `BinaryReadArchive::read_exact`'s `verify`, matching the legacy `Marshal::operator>>` short-read contract. The else branch (no matching future) dropped its throwaway `Marshal reply; reply.read_from_marshal(body, n);` — that idiom was an artifact of the legacy fd reader needing to keep its chunk list balanced; with channel-mode framing the input bytes are caller-owned and freed on return, so nothing to drain. Net effect: one Marshal allocation eliminated per inbound response on the channel demux path; the Future-facing `reply_` Marshal API is preserved for user code. Three pre-existing tangentially-related fixes folded in: (1) reordered the `set(ENABLE_BORROW_CHECKING OFF CACHE BOOL ...)` line to BEFORE `include(third-party/rusty-cpp/cmake/RustyCppSubmodule.cmake)` so mako's OFF default beats the submodule's `option(... ON)` cache race; (2) added `src/deptran` and `third-party/yaml-cpp/include` to `test_raft_srpc_transport_compile`'s include path (the test transitively pulls in `deptran/raft/srpc_transport.hpp` → `commo.h` → `__dep__.h` → `yaml-cpp/yaml.h`, none of which are propagated through `srpc`'s PUBLIC includes); (3) wrapped `srpc::likely` / `srpc::unlikely` function definitions in `src/srpc/base/debugging.hpp` with `#pragma push_macro` / `#undef` / `#pragma pop_macro` so the erpc `common.h` macros (`#define likely(x) __builtin_expect(!!(x), 1)`) don't textually substitute into the function declarations when both headers reach the same TU (e.g. via `mako/lib/srpc_rpc_backend.h` pulling in `lib/configuration.h` → erpc's common.h, then `srpc/srpc.hpp`). Added a `std::shared_ptr<srpc::SerializableProxy> serializable_` field to MarshallDeputy, populated eagerly inside `set_marshallable(shared_ptr<Marshallable>)` via `as_serializable(inner_sp_data_)` (the bridge helper from `marshal_serializable_bridge.hpp`).  Stored as `shared_ptr<SerializableProxy>` rather than `SerializableProxy` directly because `pro::proxy<SerializableFacade>` is non-copyable by default (`copyability == constraint_level::none`) — without the indirection, MarshallDeputy would become move-only and break the ~25 `req.cmd = md;` copy-assign call sites across `commo.cc` files (paxos, raft, fpga_raft, copilot, mencius, mongodb, rule, communicator, rcc, janus).  SBO inside the proxy plus shared_ptr aliasing keeps the per-deputy overhead at one heap allocation.  The body of `set_marshallable(shared_ptr<Marshallable>)` moved out-of-line to `marshal.cpp` because `as_serializable(...)` lives in `marshal_serializable_bridge.hpp`, which itself depends on `marshal.hpp`; the `.cpp` already pulls in `../srpc.hpp` (which exports the bridge) so the call is reachable without an include cycle.  Wire format unchanged — `operator<<` / `operator>>` on Marshal still drive `inner_sp_data_->to_marshal` / `from_marshal` directly; the `serializable_` field is parallel storage, populated for every set_marshallable call (including the wire-decode path through `create_actual_object_from`).  Also explicitly defaulted MarshallDeputy's copy/move ctors and assignment operators because the user-declared `~MarshallDeputy() = default;` (per [class.copy.ctor]/8) suppresses the implicit move ctor / move assignment — without these defaults, `MarshallDeputy b = std::move(a)` falls back to copy semantics, leaving `a.serializable_` non-null after the move (caught during testing by the `MoveTransfersSerializableProxy` test).  Phase 3f-3 / Phase 5 will evaluate collapsing to single-field storage once call sites have shifted off `inner()`.  8 new tests in `test_rpc_marshal_archive` covering each population path, byte-equivalence with `inner()->to_marshal`, wire-format invariance, copy-shares-proxy, and move-transfers-proxy: `PopulatedFromMarshallableCtor`, `PopulatedFromSetMarshallable`, `PopulatedFromSerializableCtor`, `BytesMatchInnerToMarshal`, `WireFormatUnchanged`, `PopulatedAfterWireDecode`, `CopyShareSerializableProxy`, `MoveTransfersSerializableProxy`.  85 tests in test_rpc_marshal_archive (77 existing + 8 new). The original 3f-3 description hedged between "single-field storage" and "lazy cache"; landed the lazy variant per the trailing recommendation, and per the `inner()` reference-stability concern (many call sites take `md.inner()` by reference and expect stable identity, which a swap to `serializable_` as primary would break).  Changed `set_marshallable(shared_ptr<Marshallable>)` in `marshal.cpp` to leave `serializable_` null instead of eagerly constructing a SerializableProxy.  Added a public `SerializableProxy& serializable() const` accessor (declared in marshal.hpp, defined in marshal.cpp) that lazily constructs the proxy on first call via `as_serializable(inner_sp_data_)` and caches the result; subsequent calls return the cached proxy.  Marked the field `mutable std::shared_ptr<srpc::SerializableProxy>` so the const accessor can populate it.  Wire format unchanged — `operator<<` / `operator>>` on Marshal still drive `inner_sp_data_->to_marshal` / `from_marshal` directly, never touching `serializable_`.  Lazy semantics extend to the wire-decode path: after `m_in >> md`, `md.serializable_` is still null until somebody calls `md.serializable()`.  Net effect: deputies that never go through the proxy-shaped accessor (which is currently every production path — no production caller reads `serializable_` yet) pay zero proxy-construction cost; the SerializableMarshallableAdapter entry path (already-Serializable types) avoids stacked `M→S→M→S` adapter wrapping when nobody reads. Phase 3f-2 was a footstep: the field now exists for future call sites, but its overhead is opt-in.  10 tests in `MarshallDeputySerializableField` (renamed from Phase 3f-2's eager-population set): `FieldStartsNullAfterCtor`, `FieldStartsNullAfterSetMarshallable`, `FieldStartsNullForSerializableCtor`, `AccessorPopulatesAndCaches` (first call populates + subsequent calls share), `AccessorBytesMatchInnerToMarshal`, `WireFormatUnchanged`, `FieldStaysLazyAfterWireDecode`, `CopyBeforeAccessLeavesBothLazy` (independent populate after copy), `CopyAfterAccessSharesCache` (shared_ptr semantics), `MoveTransfersCachedProxy`, `MoveBeforeAccessLeavesBothLazy`. Full RPC suite green: 103/107 passing under `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`, same 4 pre-existing failures (`test_rpc_timeout_retry`, `test_rpc_validation`, `test_rpc_metrics`, `rpcbench` path issue).
    - [x] **Phase 3f-4 — Wire bridge `operator<<(BinaryWriteArchive&, MarshallDeputy&)` through `md.serializable()`** ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**. First production caller of the Phase 3f-3 lazy `serializable()` accessor.  Replaced `auto serial = as_serializable(md); serial->save(ar);` with `md.serializable()->save(ar);` in `marshal_serializable_bridge.hpp`. Wire bytes unchanged — `serializable()` lazily wraps the same `inner_sp_data_` via `as_serializable(...)` on first call and caches the proxy; subsequent serializations of the same deputy reuse the cache. Per-call savings: zero allocations on the hot path after the first serialize (vs one heap allocation per fresh `MarshallableSerializableAdapter` previously). Validates the lazy-cache design pattern in production and gives the `serializable_` field its first non-test consumer.  Also retires the production caller of the deputy-overload `as_serializable(const MarshallDeputy&)`; the helper itself stays in the bridge as a documented external utility (still exercised by the `AsSerializableMarshallDeputy.ViewSavesUnderlyingMarshallableBytes` test).  Full RPC suite green: 103/107 passing under `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`, same 4 pre-existing failures.
    - [x] **Phase 3f-5 — `serializable()` short-circuit through `SerializableMarshallableAdapter`** ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**.  Optimization on the Phase 3f-3 lazy accessor: when `inner_sp_data_` is a `SerializableMarshallableAdapter` (the production path for every Phase 4-migrated type — entered via `as_marshallable(proxy)`), `serializable()` now aliases the SMA's inner proxy member directly instead of building a stacked `MarshallableSerializableAdapter` wrapping the SMA.  Pre-3f-5 behaviour wrapped twice: `serializable_ = make_shared<SerializableProxy>(as_serializable(inner_sp_data_))` produced an `M→S→M→S` stacked proxy whose `save(ar)` drove bytes through a temporary Marshal and drained them into `ar` (one extra copy per payload byte plus a fresh `MarshallableSerializableAdapter` heap allocation per first read).  Phase 3f-5 detects the SMA case via `dynamic_pointer_cast<SerializableMarshallableAdapter>(inner_sp_data_)` and uses `shared_ptr`'s aliasing constructor to point at `sma->proxy_mut()` while sharing ownership with the SMA — no extra heap allocation, no stacked-adapter byte copying, wire format identical.  Fallback path (legacy non-SMA Marshallable subclass — only test fixtures and the `CmdData` family hit this in practice) keeps the prior `as_serializable(...)`-built `MarshallableSerializableAdapter` cache.  3 new tests: `ShortCircuitsThroughSerializableMarshallableAdapter` (verifies via `proxy_cast<T>` that the cached proxy holds T directly), `ShortCircuitFallsBackForLegacyMarshallable` (verifies the fallback path for non-SMA types still works and produces correct bytes), `ShortCircuitBytesByteForByteIdenticalToFreshAsSerializable` (verifies bytes match between the cached short-circuit path and the pre-3f-5 fresh `as_serializable(md.inner())` path).  Full RPC suite green: 103/107 passing under `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`, same 4 pre-existing failures.
    - [x] **Phase 3f-prep — `MarshallDeputy` archive operator overloads** ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-28**. Added `operator<<(BinaryWriteArchive&, const MarshallDeputy&)` and `operator>>(BinaryReadArchive&, MarshallDeputy&)` in `marshal_serializable_bridge.hpp`, byte-for-byte equivalent to the legacy `Marshal&` operator pair. Write side delegates through the M→S adapter chain (`as_serializable(md)->save(ar)` after writing the kind prefix). Read side requires the archive's source to be a `MarshalSource` (detected via `proxy_cast<MarshalSourceAdapter>(*archive.source())`); recovers the underlying `Marshal*` and calls the legacy `operator>>(Marshal&, MarshallDeputy&)`. Required because the MarshallDeputy wire format has no length prefix at the payload-bytes layer, so streaming-from-arbitrary-source can't know when from_marshal has finished consuming. The common case (RPC framework reads from `Request::m` via `MarshalSource`) is satisfied. Enabled by adding `pro::skills::indirect_rtti` to `SinkFacade` and `SourceFacade`, plus exposing the proxy via `BinaryWriteArchive::sink()` / `BinaryReadArchive::source()` accessors and `MarshalSinkAdapter::sink()` / `MarshalSourceAdapter::source()` accessors. Unblocks Phase 4a-3 (TpcPrepareCommand, TpcCommitCommand, TpcBatchCommand) and other types with nested MarshallDeputy fields. 3 new tests (byte-equivalent write vs Marshal, MarshalSource round-trip, nested-deputy in user struct's save/load). 73 tests in `test_rpc_marshal_archive`. Full RPC suite green: 14 binaries, 294 tests, no regressions.

The factory registered with `MarshallDeputy::reg_initializer(kind, fn)` creates a fresh `SerializableProxy<T>`, wraps it as a `Marshallable` via `as_marshallable` / `SerializableMarshallableAdapter`, and builds a `MarInitializerState` directly. Together with the Phase 3a `MarshalSink`/`MarshalSource` bridges, this means a Serializable type T can flow through the existing `MarshallDeputy::operator<<` / `operator>>` paths byte-for-byte identically to a Marshallable subclass with the same fields. Unblocks Phase 4: per-command migrations can flip individual types from Marshallable to Serializable using `reg_serializable_in_deputy<T>` instead of `MarshallDeputy::reg_initializer<T>`. 2 new tests verify the factory is registered correctly and that an end-to-end MarshallDeputy round-trip via the new path re-produces identical bytes. 63 tests in `test_rpc_marshal_archive`. Full RPC suite green: 14 binaries, 284 tests, no regressions.

Required new infrastructure: free-function archive operators for the underlying types (mirroring the existing Marshal-based pairs byte-for-byte): (1) `mdb::Value` in `marshal-value.h/.cc` — tagged-union encoding `i64 ver | i32 tag | payload`; (2) `TxWorkspace` in `procedure.h/.cc` — `set<int32_t> keys | per-present-key (k, value) pairs | terminator -1`; (3) `SimpleCommand` in `command_marshaler.h/.cc` — 8 inherited CmdData fields (id, type, inn_id, root_id, root_type, client_id, cmd_id_in_client, rule_mode flag) followed by 6 SimpleCommand-specific fields (input as TxWorkspace, output as map<int32_t, Value>, output_size, partition_id_, timestamp_, rank_). Migration: VecPieceData gained `kMarshallKind` static + `kind()`/`save()`/`load()` methods; legacy `to_marshal`/`from_marshal` removed. Removed `TypedMarshallableAdapterTraits<VecPieceData>` specialization in `procedure.h`; switched registration from `MarshallDeputy::reg_initializer<VecPieceData>` to `srpc::reg_serializable_in_deputy<VecPieceData>` in `procedure.cc`. ONE test site updated: `DeptranVecPieceDataUsesTypedAdapterPath` switched from `MarshallDeputy(payload)` to `MarshallDeputy(wrap_serializable_aliased(payload))` to keep the in-memory shared_ptr identity check (the value-semantic `wrap_serializable` path used by Phase 4d-prep makes a copy through the proxy). Production cast sites in `fpga_raft/server.cc:448`, `raft/server.h:632`, `mongodb_connection_thread_pool.h:78`, `service.cc:300`, `raft/raft_worker.cc:609,644`, `raft/server.cc:2614` continue to use `marshallable_cast<VecPieceData>` and route through the Phase 4a-prep bridge transparently. New round-trip test `DeptranVecPieceDataNonEmptyRoundTrip` in `rpc_marshallable_proxy_test.cc` exercises the full archive operator stack end-to-end (2 SimpleCommands with TxWorkspace input, output map<int32_t, Value> with i32/i64/string variants). Full RPC suite green: 55 binaries, 720+ tests, no regressions.
    - [x] **Phase 4d-7 — `LogEntry` + `BulkPaxosCmd`** ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-29**. Both types previously used `TypedPaxosLogEnvelopeAdapter` which forwarded `entity_size`/`write_to_fd` for a `bypass_to_socket_` zero-copy fast path. Audit confirmed no caller ever sets `bypass_to_socket_=true` — the fast path is dead code. Migration: `LogEntry` adds `kind()`/`save()`/`load()` (implementations live in `paxos_worker.cc` because they reference the file-static `shared_ptr_apprch` flag); `BulkPaxosCmd` adds inline `kind()`/`save()`/`load()` directly in `paxos_worker.h`. Wire format byte-for-byte preserved: LogEntry encodes `int length | std::string` (mirrors the legacy `shared_ptr_apprch` branch that copies operation_test bytes via a temporary string); BulkPaxosCmd encodes `int32_t leader_id | slots size + N x slotid_t | ballots size + N x ballot_t | cmds size + N x MarshallDeputy bytes` (nested deputies via the Phase 3f-prep archive operators). Removed `TypedMarshallableAdapterTraits<LogEntry>` and `TypedMarshallableAdapterTraits<BulkPaxosCmd>` specializations in `paxos_worker.h`; switched registrations in `paxos_worker.cc` from `MarshallDeputy::reg_initializer<T>` to `srpc::reg_serializable_in_deputy<T>`. NO production call site updates needed — the dozens of `marshallable_cast<LogEntry>(...)` / `marshallable_cast<BulkPaxosCmd>(...)` and `make_shared<BulkPaxosCmd>()` / `MarshallDeputy(...)` and `set_marshallable(...)` sites in `paxos/server.cc`, `paxos/coordinator.cc`, `paxos/service.cc`, `paxos_worker.cc` route transparently through the Phase 4d-prep bridge dispatch. The dead `bypass_to_socket_` / `entity_size` / `write_to_fd` / `serialize_slots_ballots` / `length_as_v64` machinery is left in place pending Phase 5 cleanup. The `TypedPaxosLogEnvelopeAdapter` template is now unused — Phase 5 can remove it. Existing `PaxosLogEntryRoundTripUsesTypedAdapter` + `PaxosBulkPaxosCmdRoundTripUsesTypedAdapter` tests in `rpc_marshallable_proxy_test.cc` exercise the new path end-to-end. Full RPC suite green: 55 binaries, 705 tests, no regressions.

The static helpers (`GetCmdID`, `GetCurrentMsTime`, `Conflict`, etc.) are unaffected. No call sites materialize a SimpleRWCommand into a MarshallDeputy in current production code (the registration is for in-case fallback), so no construction sites needed updates. Full RPC suite green: 55 binaries, 719 tests, no regressions.
    - [x] **Phase 4d-9 — `raft::LogEntry` archive migration** ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-30**. Last remaining `to_marshal`/`from_marshal` pair on a non-Marshallable production struct in deptran.  `raft::LogEntry` (in `src/deptran/raft/log_storage.hpp`) is the unified Raft/Paxos log-entry struct: 6 primitive fields + nested `MarshallDeputy command`.  It is NOT a Marshallable subclass — just a struct with member methods named `to_marshal`/`from_marshal` used exclusively by the RocksDB-backed log-storage layer.  Migration: dropped the legacy `Marshal& to_marshal(Marshal&) const` / `from_marshal(Marshal&)` member pair; added `void save(BinaryWriteArchive&) const` / `void load(BinaryReadArchive&)` with byte-for-byte equivalent semantics (using the existing primitive archive operators + the Phase 3f-prep nested-MarshallDeputy archive op).  Two callers updated in `src/deptran/raft/rocksdb_log_storage.hpp`: `serialize_entry` builds `MarshalSink + BinaryWriteArchive` over a backing Marshal and calls `entry.save(writer)`; `deserialize_entry` builds `MarshalSource + BinaryReadArchive` and calls `out->load(reader)`.  The MarshalSource dependency is required by the Phase 3f-prep MarshallDeputy archive op (no length prefix at the deputy payload layer).  Also dropped the `const_cast<LogEntry&>(entry)` workaround in `serialize_entry` — `save` is `const`-qualified so no cast needed.  No other callers existed (`grep` for `entry.to_marshal` / `out->from_marshal` returned exactly the 2 sites).  Wire format byte-for-byte unchanged: existing rocksdb persistence tests (`test_rpc_rocksdb_log_storage`, ~38 tests) exercise the round-trip end-to-end and validate compatibility.  Full RPC suite green: 103/107 passing under `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`, same 4 pre-existing failures.

Six removals span the same layered set of files as Phase 4e-26: (1) `src/deptran/rcc_rpc.rpc` — `defer BulkPrepare2(...)` declaration under `MultiPaxos`. (2) `src/deptran/rcc_rpc.h` (regenerated) — `RpcBulkPrepare2Request`/`Response` envelope, abstract pure-virtual decl, async client proxy, dispatch table entry (~180 LOC). (3) `src/deptran/paxos/coordinator.{h,cc}` — `BulkCoordinatorMultiPaxos::Prepare()` decl + body (~70 LOC). (4) `src/deptran/paxos/commo.{h,cc}` — `BroadcastPrepare2` decl + body (~36 LOC; body was already `verify(0)`-then-commented-out shell). (5) `src/deptran/paxos/service.{h,cc}` — `BulkPrepare2(MarshallDeputy, ...)` N-arg overload + typed-rpc shim override (~17 LOC). (6) `src/deptran/paxos/server.{h,cc}` — `OnBulkPrepare2` decl + body (~85 LOC). The other paxos RPCs are unaffected; the `BulkCoordinatorMultiPaxos::Accept()` and `Commit()` methods remain alive (still used by `SendSyncLog`'s post-prepare reconciliation path). Net: ~440 LOC removed across 10 files (~135 LOC of hand-authored deletion). Full RPC suite green: 103/107 passing under `./docker_build.sh ci srpcTests`, same 4 pre-existing failures. No regressions.

  - [x] **L7 — Dual-envelope architecture: closed-set `TypeList` + open-set `AnyMessage`** ✅ **LANDED 2026-05-02**.  Replaces ad-hoc per-type kind constants (`MarshallDeputy::EMPTY_GRAPH=1`, `RCC_GRAPH=2`, etc.) and the earlier hash-of-typeid POC with two complementary patterns mirroring Rust serde / Protobuf:
    - **Closed-set TypeList** (already landed in the prior L6 TypeList POC):  user declares one `srpc::TypeList<...>` of every polymorphic Serializable payload, kind = 1-indexed position.  Mirrors `enum Foo { A, B, ... }` + bincode discriminants and protobuf `oneof`.  Right for command types where the universe is known up-front and wire compactness matters.
    - **Open-set AnyMessage** (this leaf):  envelope class `srpc::AnyMessage` in `src/srpc/misc/any_message.{hpp,cpp}` carries `[v64 string: type_name][payload bytes]` inside the single `MarshallDeputy::ANY_MESSAGE=24` kind.  Runtime `AnyMessageRegistry` maps name → factory and `type_index` → name (first-registered wins for `pack<T>`; multiple names per T allowed via `pack_as`).  Mirrors Rust `typetag` and protobuf `google.protobuf.Any`.  API: `pack<T>(val)` / `pack_as<T>(name, val)` / `unpack<T>()` / `is_a<T>()` / `is_a(name)` / `try_cast(MarshallDeputy)`.  Aliasing semantics: mutations on caller's `shared_ptr<T>` after `pack` remain visible to the encoded payload (uses `wrap_serializable_aliased` internally for Serializable T).
    - **Migrated payloads**:  `EmptyGraph` and `RccGraph` moved off the closed-set TypeList (where they were the only entries) onto AnyMessage with names `"janus.EmptyGraph"` and `"janus.RccGraph"`.  Their `kMarshallKind` constant + `Serializable<T, AllPayloads>` CRTP base are gone; the `AllPayloads` TypeList retired (no closed-set members).  Updated dispatch sites in `service.cc` + `janus/commo.cc` + `rcc/commo.cc` from `if (md.kind_ == EmptyGraph::static_kind()) ... else if (md.kind_ == MarshallDeputy::RCC_GRAPH) ...` to `auto am = AnyMessage::try_cast(md); if (am->is_a<EmptyGraph>()) ... else if (auto p = am->unpack<RccGraph>()) ...`.  Tests: new `test_rpc_any_message` (7 tests covering pack/unpack roundtrip, wrong-type unpack returns nullptr, wire roundtrip through MarshallDeputy, ad-hoc names via `pack_as`); `rpc_marshallable_proxy_test.cc` graph tests rewritten to use the AnyMessage envelope.
    - **Naming convention**: closed-set names are user-defined (e.g. `RaftCommands` TypeList).  Open-set names are framework-fixed at `AnyMessage` and registered by users with their own canonical strings (e.g. `"janus.RccGraph"`).
    - **Net**: +191 LOC (any_message.hpp), +135 LOC (any_message.cpp), +186 LOC (rpc_any_message_test.cc), +49/-40 LOC across deptran call sites, ~+45 LOC docs (srpc-book.md AnyMessage section).  Removes `MarshallDeputy::EMPTY_GRAPH`/`RCC_GRAPH` enum entries (kept gap comment for wire-compat).  Full RPC suite green: same 5 baseline failures (`test_rpc_docs_snippet_compile`, `test_rpc_timeout_retry`, `test_rpc_validation`, `test_rpc_metrics`, `rpcbench`), no regressions; previously-flaky `test_rpc_marshallable_proxy` now passes consistently.
  - [x] **L8 — Closed-set Command types: TypeList migration** ✅ **LANDED 2026-05-02**.  19 closed-set Serializable Command types (`LogEntry`, `TpcPrepareCommand`, `TpcCommitCommand`, `VecPieceData`, `BulkPaxosCmd`, `BulkPrepareLog`, `HeartBeatLog`, `SyncLogRequest`, `SyncLogResponse`, `SyncNoOpRequest`, `PaxosPrepCmd`, `TpcEmptyCommand`, `TpcNoopCommand`, `TpcBatchCommand`, `VecRecData`, `ViewData`, `SimpleRWCommand`, `KeyCmdBatchData`, `ReplicatedDBCommand`) migrated from manual `static constexpr int32_t kMarshallKind = MarshallDeputy::CMD_X` constants onto the central `janus::MakoCommands` TypeList in `src/deptran/mako_commands.h`.  Each type now inherits `srpc::Serializable<T, MakoCommands>` (provides `kind()` / `static_kind()` from TypeList position); the per-type `kMarshallKind` constants and matching `kind()` methods are gone.  Registrations switched from `reg_serializable_in_deputy<T>(kKind)` to the no-arg form `reg_serializable_in_deputy<T>()` which auto-derives the kind from `static_kind()`.  Dispatch sites that compared `md.kind_ == MarshallDeputy::CMD_X` literals updated to `T::static_kind()`.  Tautological `verify(T::kMarshallKind == MarshallDeputy::CMD_X)` lines deleted.  `MarshallDeputy::Kind` enum: dropped `CMD_TPC_PREPARE..CMD_REPLICATED_DB` (values 4-23 with a gap at 17); kept `UNKNOWN=0`, `CONTAINER_CMD=3` (used by legacy `CmdData` Marshallable base, never wire-round-trips), and `ANY_MESSAGE=24`.  Wire-format break (kind values shift from {3,4,...,23} with gaps to {1,2,...,19} TypeList positions); paired with L9.  Net: 27 files, +274/-315 LOC.  Full RPC suite green: same baseline failures.
  - [x] **L9 — Wire compaction: `int32 kind_` → `v32`** ✅ **LANDED 2026-05-02**.  `MarshallDeputy::kind_` now serializes as `srpc::v32` (variable-length SparseInt: 1 byte for values in [-64, 63] — SparseInt's first-byte encoding has a 6-bit signed payload — up to 5 bytes for full int32) instead of raw 4-byte `int32_t`.  Both wire paths (`operator<<(Marshal&, MarshallDeputy&)` in `marshal.hpp:1494-1521` and `operator<<(BinaryWriteArchive&, MarshallDeputy&)` in `marshal_serializable_bridge.hpp:448-466`) updated.  After L8's TypeList migration every closed-set kind is in [1, 19] and ANY_MESSAGE=24, all in the 1-byte range — so every production polymorphic envelope saves 3 bytes for the kind prefix.  Hot-path effect (every Paxos LogEntry, TpcCommit, TpcEmpty heartbeat, RccGraph reply, etc.) at a few hundred log entries per second per follower is non-trivial network savings under load.  New test `MarshallDeputyKindEncoding.V32SingleByteFor*` (in `rpc_marshal_archive_test`) pins the wire-size guarantee with kind=50: encoded length = 1 (v32 kind) + 4 (int32 payload) = 5 bytes, plus a round-trip recovery check.  Wire-format break (combined with L8); cluster needs simultaneous redeployment.  Net: 3 files, +102/-3 LOC.  Full RPC suite green: same baseline failures.

  - **`MarshallDeputy::inner_sp_data_` goes away; only the proxy field remains** — **OBSOLETE 2026-05-03**.  The Phase 3f-3 lazy `serializable_` proxy cache (mutable shared_ptr populated on first `serializable()` access) was retired in L10d-prep along with its only production caller, the bridge `operator<<(BinaryWriteArchive&, MarshallDeputy&)` fast path.  After L10c, production code lives on `janus::Command`, which stores a single `shared_ptr<Marshallable> inner_` (matching the `MarshallDeputy::inner_sp_data_` shape so call sites can keep using `cmd.inner_marshallable()`) and lazily probes the proxy via `serializable_cast<T>` on unpack — no parallel proxy-cache field, no dual storage.  The dual-storage problem this DoD bullet was originally about no longer applies; the lingering question is the Marshallable base itself, folded into L10f.
  - **Estimated −800 LOC** — stale; no longer represents an actionable target since both bullets above are blocked / rejected.  The realised Phase 5 cleanup totals as of 2026-04-30 are ~1260 LOC across 5a-1 + 5b-1..5b-12 (per the Phase 5b-10 / 5b-11 retirement notes).

### DoD
- [~] Each user-defined command type has ONE `template<Archive> void serialize(Archive&)` method (or save+load pair only when asymmetric). **PARTIAL — every migrated type has a `save`/`load`/`kind` pair (the equivalent of cereal's `serialize` split for asymmetric write/read paths). No type uses the single-method `template<Archive>` form yet; the codebase committed to the explicit save+load shape during Phase 1 to allow asymmetric stream consumption (`SerializableProxy::save` writes payload bytes only — the kind framing is at the `MarshallDeputy` layer).**
- [~] `MarshallDeputy` has one field for the carrier (the proxy), one for the kind tag — no shared_ptr mirror. **REVISED 2026-04-30 after Phase 3f-3 landed**: post-3f-3 `MarshallDeputy` carries `inner_sp_data_` (shared_ptr<Marshallable>, primary) AND `serializable_` (mutable shared_ptr<SerializableProxy>, lazy cache populated on first `serializable()` accessor call), plus `kind_`.  The "no shared_ptr mirror" goal in this DoD is rejected per Phase 3f-3's documented decision: ~30 production call sites take `md.inner()` BY REFERENCE and rely on stable identity, so we can't drop `inner_sp_data_` or make it derived without an invasive sweep.  The lazy `serializable_` cache means deputies that never go through the proxy-shaped accessor pay zero proxy-construction cost — the field is parallel storage (not a mirror), with opt-in overhead.  The DoD is effectively reframed as "MarshallDeputy has an explicit primary inner-data field plus an opt-in proxy view"; both halves are achieved by Phase 3f-2 + 3f-3.
- [x] `Marshal::write_to_fd` is gone; callers compose `BufferSink` (or use `FdSink` directly). ✅ **LANDED 2026-04-29** at Phase 5b-7 (write side) + Phase 5b-11 (companion read-side `Marshal::read_from_fd` removal).
- [x] Adding a new transport (e.g. RDMA) is a new Sink class + tests; no command-type code changes. ✅ **STRUCTURALLY DONE** since Phase 1a — `SinkFacade`/`SourceFacade` proxies are runtime-polymorphic; `BufferSink`/`FdSink`/`MarshalSink` are the existing sinks. New transports plug in by adding a Sink adapter class.
- [x] Adding a new format (e.g. JSON debug dumps) is a new Archive class; no command-type code changes. ✅ **STRUCTURALLY DONE** since Phase 1a — Archive operates over the Sink/Source proxies; primitives + containers route through `operator<<`/`operator>>` overloads. A JSON archive would be a new class providing those operators.
- [x] Wire format byte layout is byte-for-byte unchanged across the migration (verified at every phase). ✅ **VERIFIED** — every migration leaf (Phase 4a-1..4d-8, Phase 4c-1) explicitly states "wire format byte-for-byte identical" and every Phase 5b cleanup leaf preserves wire format. `test_rpc_marshal_archive`'s byte-compat tests (Phase 1) and the round-trip suite (Phase 4d-6 nested-deputy / Phase 4d-7 LogEntry+BulkPaxosCmd / Phase 4c-1 ReplicatedDBCommand) are the standing wire-format guards.

### Out of scope
- Switching the wire format — preservation of byte-for-byte compatibility is mandatory.
- Removing `std::shared_ptr<Marshallable>` from public APIs — that's L6 and remains blocked on the non-owning-shared_ptr pattern in `scheduler.cc:375` and the persisted log format in `LogEntry::command`.
- `rusty::Arc` adoption — separate concern; can be layered atop the new design later without touching command-type serialization.
